1 Kings cross-references

1 Kings 1:1–13:34 · 524 completed verses · 543 completed pairs

Initial curated pass. Next: 1 Kings 14:21. Later source verses remain pending. Greek-based Saints-Translation numbering is retained.

Completed means the initial curated cross-reference pass: the source verse and every listed target passage were read in the pinned Saints-Translation English records, and each retained pair has a specific explanation. It does not mean every possible biblical connection has been exhausted, or independent scholarly approval.

1 Kings 1:1

King David was old and advanced in days; and they wrapped him in garments, but he did not grow warm.

  1. 1 Chronicles 23:1 David advanced in days before the succession notices

    Both describe David as old or full of days; Chronicles additionally states that he made Solomon king, whereas Kings begins with his lack of warmth, preserving the different supplied notices.

    Read matching passage

    1 Chronicles 23:1 David was old and full of days, and he made Solomon his son king in his place over Israel.

    Pinned source record

1 Kings 1:2

His attendants said, “Let them seek a young virgin for our lord the king. She shall attend the king and keep him warm; she shall lie with him, and our lord the king will become warm.”

  1. 1 Kings 1:3-4 Same-chapter the proposed attendance and warming arrangement and its narrated outcome

    The attendants propose finding a young woman to attend and warm David; the following account names Abishag and reports her service and warming, without reconstructing a medical diagnosis or adding an unreported marriage.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 1:3 And they sought a fair young woman throughout all the territory of Israel, and found Abishag the Shunammite, and brought her to the king.

    1 Kings 1:4 The young woman was very fair; she kept the king warm and served him, but the king did not know her.

    Pinned source record

1 Kings 1:3

And they sought a fair young woman throughout all the territory of Israel, and found Abishag the Shunammite, and brought her to the king.

  1. 1 Kings 2:17 The exact Abishag the Shunammite designation in attendance and marriage-request scenes

    The search brings Abishag the Shunammite to David; Adonijah later asks that same named woman and descriptor as a wife, preserving a request rather than claiming the requested marriage occurred.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 2:17 He said to her, “Please speak to King Solomon—for he will not turn his face away from you—and he will give me Abishag the Shunammite as a wife.”

  1. 2 Samuel 15:1 Royal sons preparing chariots and fifty advance runners

1 Kings 1:6

His father had never restrained him by saying, “Why have you done this?” He too was very handsome in appearance, and his mother bore him after Absalom.

  1. 1 Samuel 3:13 Different paternal failures to restrain or admonish sons

    Kings says David never restrained Adonijah by asking why he acted so; the word concerning Eli says he did not admonish sons who reviled God, comparing the stated lack of rebuke without equating their offenses or supplying a psychological cause.

    The old prophet claims an angel authorized the return meal, and the narrator explicitly says he lied. The later Lord's word judges the return and eating as defiance of the original command; the comparison treats the angel report as a reported lie, not as a verified angel's instruction.
